=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a city divided into H \times W blocks in the north-south-east-west directions, and there is exactly one building in each block.
Specifically, in the block at the i-th row from the north (1\leq i\leq H) and the j-th column from the west (1\leq j\leq W) (hereafter referred to as block (i,j)), there is a building of F_{i,j} floors.
Takahashi has two ways of moving. If he is on the X-th floor (1\leq X\leq F_{i,j}) of the building in block (i,j), he can:

- Move up or down one floor within the same building using stairs. If X=1, he cannot move down; if X=F_{i,j}, he cannot move up.
- Choose a building with at least X floors in a cardinally adjacent block, and move to the X-th floor of that building using a (sky) walkway.

Here, two blocks (i,j) and (i',j') are cardinally adjacent if and only if \lvert i - i'\rvert + \lvert j - j'\rvert = 1.
You are given Q queries to be answered. The i-th query (1\leq i\leq Q) is the following.

Find the minimum possible number of times that Takahashi uses stairs to move from the Y_i-th floor of the building in block (A_i,B_i) to the Z_i-th floor of the building in block (C_i,D_i).
The count of times using stairs is incremented each time he moves up or down one floor, possibly multiple times within the same building. (For example, moving from the 1st floor to the 6th floor of a building counts as 5 uses of stairs.)
Note that he does not have to minimize the number of times he uses walkways.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
H W
F_{1,1} F_{1,2} \ldots F_{1,W}
F_{2,1} F_{2,2} \ldots F_{2,W}
\vdots
F_{H,1} F_{H,2} \ldots F_{H,W}
Q
A_1 B_1 Y_1 C_1 D_1 Z_1
A_2 B_2 Y_2 C_2 D_2 Z_2
\vdots
A_Q B_Q Y_Q C_Q D_Q Z_Q

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th query as an integer.

Constraints


- 1\leq H \leq 500
- 1\leq W \leq 500
- 1\leq F_{i,j} \leq 10^6
- 1\leq Q\leq 2\times 10^5
- 1\leq A_i,C_i\leq H
- 1\leq B_i,D_i\leq W
- 1\leq Y_i\leq F_{A_i,B_i}
- 1\leq Z_i\leq F_{C_i,D_i}
- (A_i,B_i,Y_i)\neq (C_i,D_i,Z_i)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 3
12 10 6
1 1 3
8 6 7
2
1 1 10 3 1 6
1 1 6 1 2 4

Sample Output 1

10
2

For the first query, for example, it is possible to move from the 10th floor of the building in block (1,1) to the 6th floor of the building in block (3,1) by using stairs a total of 10 times, in the following manner:

- Move from the 10th floor of the building in block (1,1) to the 10th floor of the building in block (1,2) via a walkway.
- Use stairs 4 times to go from the 10th floor down to the 6th floor of the building in block (1,2).
- Move from the 6th floor of the building in block (1,2) to the 6th floor of the building in block (1,3) via a walkway.
- Use stairs 3 times to go from the 6th floor down to the 3rd floor of the building in block (1,3).
- Move from the 3rd floor of the building in block (1,3) to the 3rd floor of the building in block (2,3) via a walkway.
- Move from the 3rd floor of the building in block (2,3) to the 3rd floor of the building in block (3,3) via a walkway.
- Use stairs 3 times to go from the 3rd floor up to the 6th floor of the building in block (3,3).
- Move from the 6th floor of the building in block (3,3) to the 6th floor of the building in block (3,2) via a walkway.
- Move from the 6th floor of the building in block (3,2) to the 6th floor of the building in block (3,1) via a walkway.

It is impossible to make this journey using at most 9 uses of stairs, so we output 10.
For the second query, if you first use a walkway to go to the building in block (1,2), and then use the stairs twice to go from the 6th floor down to the 4th floor, it is possible to move from the 6th floor of the building in block (1,1) to the 4th floor of the building in block (1,2) by using the stairs twice.

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Thus need answer queries: compute M = maximum bottleneck connectivity threshold between two cells in grid with vertex weights F. H,W <=500, N=250k, Q=2e5. Need efficient.

This is classic: Build maximum spanning tree (or Kruskal reconstruction tree) on grid graph with edge weights min(F_u,F_v) or vertex weights. The maximum bottleneck path value between any two vertices equals minimum edge weight on path in a maximum spanning tree of the graph (where edge weight = min(F_u,F_v)). Alternative use Kruskal reconstruction tree with LCA, more efficient and elegant. Let's explore.

Build maximum spanning tree / Kruskal reconstruction tree over grid cells by vertex weights. We can compute M for all queries offline using DSU union tree (Kruskal reconstruction tree) in O(N log N + Q log N). Since N=250k, Q=2e5, feasible. Need implement LCA binary lifting. This might be cleaner and faster than 20-iteration DSU. Let's consider.

We have graph grid with edge weight w(u,v) = min(F_u,F_v). The maximum bottleneck path M(s,t) equals the maximum edge weight along path in a maximum spanning tree? More specifically, if we run Kruskal on edges sorted descending, union components. The value at which s and t become connected is M. We can build a Kruskal reconstruction tree (KRT) where leaves are original cells, internal nodes represent unions with value = edge weight (or vertex weight?). Then M(s,t) = value of LCA(s,t) in the KRT? Need check.

Standard KRT for maximum spanning tree: Sort edges descending. Initially each vertex separate. When processing edge (u,v) with weight w, if u,v in different components, create new node with value w, children = roots of components of u and v. Then the LCA of two original vertices in the resulting tree has value equal to the minimum edge weight on the path in the maximum spanning tree? Actually for maximum bottleneck, if using max spanning tree, M(s,t) = minimum edge weight along path in MST. In KRT built by Kruskal descending, LCA value = the weight of the edge that connects the two components at the moment they merge, which is exactly the maximum bottleneck value. Yes.

But edge weights are min(F_u,F_v). We can instead process vertices descending (like adding cells) and union active cells with active neighbors. The merge value when two components become connected via a cell of height h? If adding cells in descending F, when we add a cell v of height h, it connects all active neighbor components. The threshold at which those components become connected is h (because v has height h, and edges from v to neighbors have min(F_v,F_neighbor) = h if neighbor active with F>=h; if neighbor has F>h, edge weight h; if neighbor exactly h, h). More generally, if we process cells sorted descending by F, when adding a cell with height h, any new connections between previously separate active components through this cell occur at threshold h. We can union them, and create KRT nodes with value h? Need handle components merging multiple at once. This is like building "component tree" of upper-level sets. The LCA value in this merge tree gives M(s,t). This avoids processing edges separately (N*4 edges). Need build tree with up to N internal nodes? In standard KRT, each union creates one internal node, so number internal nodes <= N-1, total <=2N-1. But if adding a cell connects k components, we can create k-1 internal nodes (or one node with multiple children? For binary LCA, need binary tree; can create chain of unions with same value h). Total internal nodes <= N-1. Leaves N. Good.

Then answer queries by LCA value. Need handle same cell: LCA of leaf with itself is leaf, no internal value. But M=F_s? In KRT leaves don't have value? We can assign leaf value = F_cell? Since Y,Z<=F_s, answer |Y-Z|. So if LCA is leaf, set M=F_s. Or in LCA function if u==v return F[u]. Good.

Need be careful: KRT built by vertex additions with value h. Suppose two cells s,t become connected when adding a cell v with height h. The path between s and t in grid may go through v; bottleneck min along path = h (because v height h, and all other cells on path have height >= h). M = h. LCA value = h. Good.

Let's fully understand KRT construction and LCA.

KRT construction details:

Input H,W, F matrix. Flatten id = i*W+j.

Sort cells by F descending. Need list of (F, id). N=250k; sorting O(N log N) okay.

DSU arrays:
- `dsu_parent` length N, initialized -1 or self. Since we activate cells one by one. Could use parent list where inactive parent=-1. When activate, parent[v]=v.
- `comp_node` length N? For each DSU root r, store KRT node id representing the component. Since only root's value meaningful. We can use array/list length N, initialized -1. When union, set comp_node[new_root] = new_node.
- `krt_parent` list length max_nodes (2N) initialized -1. For leaves, parent initially -1; when merged, set krt_parent[leaf_or_internal_root] = new_node. For internal node, parent initially -1; when its component merges into higher, set parent. At end root has parent -1.
- `node_value` list length max_nodes, value for each node. For leaves, F[id]; for internal, h. Need maybe only internal values for LCA; leaf value for same cell. Store as list/array of int length max_nodes. Values up to 1e6, can use list of ints (pointers) or array('i').
- `left_child`, `right_child` for KRT? For LCA via binary lifting, we don't need children except to compute depth/up. We can compute depth from parent pointers without children? Need root and parent relationships. Since parent pointers form a tree from leaves up to root. We can compute depth of each node by traversing from leaves upward? Parent pointers point upward; depth = distance to root. We can compute depths for all nodes by processing nodes in creation order? Internal nodes created later have higher node_id; parent of a node is always an internal node created after it (larger node_id). Leaves have ids 0..N-1? Let's assign leaf id = original cell id? But KRT node ids should be separate from cell ids. We can use original cell id as leaf node id? Then internal node ids from N to 2N-2. DSU comp_node stores KRT node id. Original cell id = leaf node id. This saves memory. But DSU parent uses cell ids; leaf node id equals cell id. Good. So KRT node id range 0..N-1 leaves, N..2N-2 internal. `node_value` length 2N-1; for leaf id <N, value=F[id]. For internal id>=N, value=h. `krt_parent` length 2N-1.

When activating cell v (leaf id v), DSU parent[v]=v, comp_node[v]=v (leaf node id). Then for each active neighbor u:
    ru = find(u); rv = find(v)
    if ru != rv:
        new_node = next_internal_id
        node_value[new_node] = h
        krt_parent[comp_node[ru]] = new_node
        krt_parent[comp_node[rv]] = new_node
        # union DSU: attach ru under rv or rv under ru. Need choose new DSU root and set comp_node.
        dsu_parent[ru] = rv
        comp_node[rv] = new_node
        # comp_node[ru] no longer needed
But careful: `find(v)` after union? We use `rv = find(v)` before union. If we attach ru to rv, root is rv. But if later another neighbor, `find(v)` returns rv. Good. However, if we attach ru to rv, DSU parent of ru set to rv. But if rv was not root? We use find so both roots. Good.
But if we choose to attach ru to rv always, DSU tree may become unbalanced? Union by size/rank needed for performance. But if we always attach neighbor root to v root, the DSU tree could be chain? Actually v root is the component containing current cell; after union, root remains v's root. Over processing, if we always attach old components to new cell's component, DSU tree can become deep? Let's see: New cell component size 1; attach large component root to small root -> tree depth can grow O(N). Need union by size/rank. We can maintain `dsu_size` and choose root with larger size. But KRT comp_node must be associated with DSU root. If we attach rv (v's root) to ru (neighbor root), then new root is ru, and comp_node[ru]=new_node. But then `find(v)` after union? v's DSU parent points to rv which points to ru, so find(v) returns ru. That's okay. Need update `rv = find(v)` after each union? In loop, after union, we can set `rv = new_root` or just call find(v) next iteration. Simpler: in each neighbor iteration, compute ru=find(u), rv=find(v). If ru != rv, create node, set parents, union by size:
    if size[ru] < size[rv]:
        dsu_parent[ru] = rv
        size[rv] += size[ru]
        comp_node[rv] = new_node
        # root = rv
    else:
        dsu_parent[rv] = ru
        size[ru] += size[rv]
        comp_node[ru] = new_node
        # root = ru
Then next iteration find(v) returns root. Good.

Need ensure when creating internal node, its two children are `comp_node[ru]` and `comp_node[rv]` before union. These are KRT roots of the two components. Set krt_parent[child1]=new_node, krt_parent[child2]=new_node. But what if one component is the newly activated leaf v and the other is neighbor component; comp_node[rv] is leaf v (or current combined component root) before union. Good.

Potential issue: When processing a cell, we activate it first, then union with active neighbors. If a neighbor has same height and is processed later, okay. But what about two active neighbor components that are already connected through other active cells? find(u) and find(v) will be same, skip. Good.

Total internal nodes created = successful unions. Starting with zero active, after each activation component count +1, each union -1. Final active all N, components=1, so unions=N-1. But wait if grid disconnected? Grid graph connected, yes. If H*W=1, no unions, internal nodes 0, KRT root is leaf 0. Need handle.

Now LCA binary lifting.

We have `krt_parent` for all nodes (0..total_nodes-1), where root has -1. Need compute depth and up table.

Because parent pointers point from child to parent, and parent id > child id? Is it always? Leaves id 0..N-1. Internal nodes created sequentially from N upward. When we set parent of child (leaf or older internal) to new_node, new_node id > child id. Thus along any path to root, node ids strictly increase. Therefore root has largest id (total_nodes-1). This is convenient. We can compute depth for all nodes by iterating nodes in decreasing id? Since parent id > child id, if we process node ids from total_nodes-1 down to 0, depth[parent] known? Let's see: For node i, parent p > i. If we iterate i from total_nodes-1 down to 0, when at i, p has been processed (since p>i), so depth[i] = depth[p]+1. Root depth=0. This avoids DFS/children. Nice! Need root id = total_nodes-1 (last internal node) if N>1; if N=1 root=0. Since parent pointers form a tree rooted at last created node. Because every internal node except final root becomes child of later internal node; final root parent -1. So yes.

Then build up table:

LCA function:
```
def lca(u,v):
    if depth[u] < depth[v]: u,v = v,u
    diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
    bit=0
    while diff:
        if diff & 1:
            u = up[bit][u]
        diff >>= 1; bit += 1
    if u == v: return u
    for k in range(LOG-1, -1, -1):
        if up[k][u] != up[k][v]:
            u = up[k][u]; v = up[k][v]
    return up[0][u]
```
Need if u/v are leaves. Works.

Let's think about the formula answer = max(|Y-Z|, Y+Z - 2*M). Is M possibly > 1e6? No. If M < min(Y,Z), Y+Z-2M positive. If M >= min, max diff. Let's derive again:
g(m) = min_{1<=c<=m} |Y-c| + |Z-c|.
Let a=min(Y,Z), b=max(Y,Z).
If m < a: both > c, function = (Y+Z)-2c, minimized at c=m -> Y+Z-2m.
If a <= m <= b: choose c=a? For c in [a,m], |Y-c|+|Z-c| = (b-c)+(c-a)=b-a = diff. So g=diff.
If m > b: choose c between a,b, g=diff. Thus g = diff if m>=a else Y+Z-2m. This equals max(diff, Y+Z-2m)? If m>=a, Y+Z-2m <= Y+Z-2a = diff, so max=diff. If m<a, Y+Z-2m > diff? Need check: m<a => 2m < 2a = Y+Z - diff? Since a=(Y+Z-diff)/2. Then Y+Z-2m > diff. So max=that. Yes. So formula works.

Use:
```

input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
H,W = map(int,input().split())

    A,B,Y,C,D,Z = map(int,input().split())
    s = (A-1)*W + (B-1)
    t = (C-1)*W + (D-1)
    queries.append((s,t,Y,Z))
```

Now, KRT construction with arrays/lists.

Let's write code skeleton:

```
import sys
from array import array

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    H, W = map(int, input().split())
    N = H * W
    F = []
    cells = []
    for i in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        F.append(row)
        base = i * W
        for j, val in enumerate(row):
            cells.append((val, base + j))
    cells.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
    # optional del F
    dsu = [-1] * N
    sz = [0] * N
    comp = [-1] * N
    max_nodes = 2 * N
    node_value = [0] * max_nodes
    krt_parent = [-1] * max_nodes
    total_nodes = N

    # find function nested uses dsu
    def find(x):
        while dsu[x] != x:
            dsu[x] = dsu[dsu[x]]
            x = dsu[x]
        return x

    for h, v in cells:
        dsu[v] = v
        sz[v] = 1
        comp[v] = v
        node_value[v] = h
        r = v // W
        c = v - r * W
        # neighbor up
        if r > 0:
            nb = v - W
            if dsu[nb] != -1:
                ru = find(nb)
                rv = find(v)
                if ru != rv:
                    new = total_nodes
                    total_nodes += 1
                    node_value[new] = h
                    child1 = comp[ru]
                    child2 = comp[rv]
                    krt_parent[child1] = new
                    krt_parent[child2] = new
                    if sz[ru] < sz[rv]:
                        dsu[ru] = rv
                        sz[rv] += sz[ru]
                        comp[rv] = new
                    else:
                        dsu[rv] = ru
                        sz[ru] += sz[rv]
                        comp[ru] = new
        # similarly down, left, right
```
This duplicates union code. Could define helper `unite(nb, h, v)` but function call inside loop maybe overhead. Could write a small inner code repeated? To avoid duplication, we can loop over neighbor offsets:

for dr, dc in ((1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)):
    nr = r+dr; nc = c+dc
    if 0 <= nr < H and 0 <= nc < W:
        nb = nr*W + nc
        if dsu[nb] != -1:
            ...
```

Depth computation:
```
depth = array('i', [0]) * total_nodes
for i in range(total_nodes - 1, -1, -1):
    p = krt_parent[i]
    if p != -1:
        depth[i] = depth[p] + 1
    else:
        depth[i] = 0
```
Since root has p=-1 and is largest id. But what if there are multiple roots due to bug? Grid connected, no. If N=1, root id0 p=-1.

Build up:
```
LOG = total_nodes.bit_length()
up = [array('i', [0]) * total_nodes for _ in range(LOG)]
up0 = up[0]
for i in range(total_nodes):
    p = krt_parent[i]
    up0[i] = p if p != -1 else i
for k in range(1, LOG):
    prev = up[k-1]
    curr = up[k]
    for i in range(total_nodes):
        curr[i] = prev[prev[i]]
```
Potential issue: `array('i', [0]) * total_nodes` if total_nodes is 0? no. But if total_nodes large, multiplication works. However, `array('i', [0]) * total_nodes` creates array of length total_nodes with all zeros. Good.
Could use `array('i', [0]) * total_nodes` inside list comprehension; each array independent. Good.

LCA function:
```
def lca(u, v):
    if depth[u] < depth[v]:
        u, v = v, u
    diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
    bit = 0
    while diff:
        if diff & 1:
            u = up[bit][u]
        diff >>= 1
        bit += 1
    if u == v:
        return u
    for k in range(LOG - 1, -1, -1):
        if up[k][u] != up[k][v]:
            u = up[k][u]
            v = up[k][v]
    return up[0][u]
```
Need `depth` array access returns int. `up[bit][u]` okay. In the while diff, if diff has bit beyond LOG? diff <= depth max <= total_nodes-1, bit_length <= LOG, so up[bit] exists. Good.

Now, answer queries:
```
out = []
for s,t,Y,Z in queries:
    w = lca(s,t)
    m = node_value[w]
    ans = abs(Y-Z)
    val = Y + Z - 2 * m
    if val > ans:
        ans = val
    out.append(str(ans))
sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```
Need if s/t are original cell ids (leaves) and KRT node ids leaves same. Good.

Let's write KRT loop:

```
dirs = ((1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1))
for h, v in cells:
    dsu[v] = v
    sz[v] = 1
    comp[v] = v
    node_value[v] = h
    r = v // W
    c = v - r * W
    for dr, dc in dirs:
        nr = r + dr
        nc = c + dc
        if 0 <= nr < H and 0 <= nc < W:
            nb = nr * W + nc
            if dsu[nb] != -1:
                ru = find(nb)
                rv = find(v)
                if ru != rv:
                    new = total_nodes
                    total_nodes += 1
                    node_value[new] = h
                    child1 = comp[ru]
                    child2 = comp[rv]
                    krt_parent[child1] = new
                    krt_parent[child2] = new
                    if sz[ru] < sz[rv]:
                        dsu[ru] = rv
                        sz[rv] += sz[ru]
                        comp[rv] = new
                    else:

Let's outline final code:

```
def main():
    import sys
    from array import array
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    H, W = map(int, input().split())
    N = H * W
    cells = []
    for i in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        base = i * W
        for j, val in enumerate(row):
            cells.append((val, base + j))
    cells.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)

    dsu = [-1] * N
    sz = [0] * N
    comp = [-1] * N
    max_nodes = 2 * N
    node_value = array('i', [0]) * max_nodes
    krt_parent = array('i', [-1]) * max_nodes
    total_nodes = N

    def find(x):
        while dsu[x] != x:
            dsu[x] = dsu[dsu[x]]
            x = dsu[x]
        return x

    dirs = ((1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1))
    for h, v in cells:
        dsu[v] = v
        sz[v] = 1
        comp[v] = v
        node_value[v] = h
        r = v // W
        c = v - r * W
        for dr, dc in dirs:
            nr = r + dr
            nc = c + dc
            if 0 <= nr < H and 0 <= nc < W:
                nb = nr * W + nc
                if dsu[nb] != -1:
                    ru = find(nb)
                    rv = find(v)
                    if ru != rv:
                        new = total_nodes
                        total_nodes += 1
                        node_value[new] = h
                        child1 = comp[ru]
                        child2 = comp[rv]
                        krt_parent[child1] = new
                        krt_parent[child2] = new
                        if sz[ru] < sz[rv]:
                            dsu[ru] = rv
                            sz[rv] += sz[ru]
                            comp[rv] = new
                        else:
                            dsu[rv] = ru
                            sz[ru] += sz[rv]
                            comp[ru] = new

    del cells, dsu, sz, comp, find
    # build up
    depth = array('i', [0]) * total_nodes
    for i in range(total_nodes - 1, -1, -1):
        p = krt_parent[i]
        if p != -1:
            depth[i] = depth[p] + 1
        # else depth[i]=0 already
    LOG = total_nodes.bit_length()
    up = [array('i', [0]) * total_nodes for _ in range(LOG)]
    up0 = up[0]
    for i in range(total_nodes):
        p = krt_parent[i]
        up0[i] = p if p != -1 else i
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = up[k-1]
        curr = up[k]
        for i in range(total_nodes):
            curr[i] = prev[prev[i]]
    del krt_parent

    Q = int(input())
    S = [0]*Q; T=[0]*Q; Ys=[0]*Q; Zs=[0]*Q
    for qi in range(Q):
        A,B,Y,C,D,Z = map(int, input().split())
        S[qi] = (A-1)*W + (B-1)
        T[qi] = (C-1)*W + (D-1)
        Ys[qi] = Y
        Zs[qi] = Z

    def lca(u,v):
        if depth[u] < depth[v]:
            u,v = v,u
        diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
        bit = 0
        while diff:
            if diff & 1:
                u = up[bit][u]
            diff >>= 1
            bit += 1
        if u == v:
            return u
        for k in range(LOG - 1, -1, -1):
            if up[k][u] != up[k][v]:
                u = up[k][u]
                v = up[k][v]
        return up[0][u]

    out = []
    for i in range(Q):
        u = S[i]; v = T[i]
        w = lca(u, v)
        m = node_value[w]
        ans = Ys[i] - Zs[i]
        if ans < 0:
            ans = -ans
        val = Ys[i] + Zs[i] - 2 * m
        if val > ans:
            ans = val
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

Now, let's consider if LCA function uses `up` and `depth` from outer scope; in Python, reading outer variables is slightly slower than locals. We can bind them as default arguments:
```
def lca(u, v, depth=depth, up=up, LOG=LOG):
    ...
```
This makes them local? Default args are local variables. Good. Use this for speed.
Similarly, in answer loop, `node_value` local already.

Let's write lca with defaults:
```
def lca(u, v, depth=depth, up=up, LOG=LOG):
    if depth[u] < depth[v]:
        u, v = v, u
    diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
    bit = 0
    while diff:
        if diff & 1:
            u = up[bit][u]
        diff >>= 1
        bit += 1
    if u == v:
        return u
    for k in range(LOG - 1, -1, -1):
        if up[k][u] != up[k][v]:
            u = up[k][u]
            v = up[k][v]
    return up[0][u]
```
Good.

Let's think of LCA with list of lists: up table build:
```
up = [[0] * total_nodes for _ in range(LOG)]
up0 = up[0]
for i in range(total_nodes):
    p = krt_parent[i]
    up0[i] = p if p != -1 else i
for k in range(1, LOG):
    prev = up[k-1]; curr = up[k]
    for i in range(total_nodes):
        curr[i] = prev[prev[i]]
```
This is fast. Depth list:
```
depth = [0] * total_nodes
for i in range(total_nodes-1,-1,-1):
    p = krt_parent[i]
    if p != -1:
        depth[i] = depth[p] + 1
```

Now, let's write final code:

```
import sys
from array import array

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    H, W = map(int, input().split())
    N = H * W
    cells = []
    for i in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        base = i * W
        for j, val in enumerate(row):
            cells.append((val, base + j))
    cells.sort(reverse=True)

    dsu = [-1] * N
    sz = [0] * N
    comp = [-1] * N
    max_nodes = 2 * N + 5
    node_value = [0] * max_nodes
    krt_parent = [-1] * max_nodes
    total_nodes = N

    def find(x):
        while dsu[x] != x:
            dsu[x] = dsu[dsu[x]]
            x = dsu[x]
        return x

    dirs = ((1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1))
    for h, v in cells:
        dsu[v] = v
        sz[v] = 1
        comp[v] = v
        node_value[v] = h
        r = v // W
        c = v - r * W
        for dr, dc in dirs:
            nr = r + dr
            nc = c + dc
            if 0 <= nr < H and 0 <= nc < W:
                nb = nr * W + nc
                if dsu[nb] != -1:
                    ru = find(nb)
                    rv = find(v)
                    if ru != rv:
                        new = total_nodes
                        total_nodes += 1
                        node_value[new] = h
                        child1 = comp[ru]
                        child2 = comp[rv]
                        krt_parent[child1] = new
                        krt_parent[child2] = new
                        if sz[ru] < sz[rv]:
                            dsu[ru] = rv
                            sz[rv] += sz[ru]
                            comp[rv] = new
                        else:
                            dsu[rv] = ru
                            sz[ru] += sz[rv]
                            comp[ru] = new

    del cells, dsu, sz, comp, find

    depth = array('i', [0]) * total_nodes
    for i in range(total_nodes - 1, -1, -1):
        p = krt_parent[i]
        if p != -1:
            depth[i] = depth[p] + 1

    LOG = total_nodes.bit_length()
    up = [array('i', [0]) * total_nodes for _ in range(LOG)]
    up0 = up[0]
    for i in range(total_nodes):
        p = krt_parent[i]
        up0[i] = p if p != -1 else i
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = up[k - 1]
        curr = up[k]
        for i in range(total_nodes):
            curr[i] = prev[prev[i]]

    del krt_parent

    Q = int(input())
    S = [0] * Q
    T = [0] * Q
    Ys = [0] * Q
    Zs = [0] * Q
    for qi in range(Q):
        A, B, Y, C, D, Z = map(int, input().split())
        S[qi] = (A - 1) * W + (B - 1)
        T[qi] = (C - 1) * W + (D - 1)
        Ys[qi] = Y
        Zs[qi] = Z

    def lca(u, v, depth=depth, up=up, LOG=LOG):
        if depth[u] < depth[v]:
            u, v = v, u
        diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
        bit = 0
        while diff:
            if diff & 1:
                u = up[bit][u]
            diff >>= 1
            bit += 1
        if u == v:
            return u
        for k in range(LOG - 1, -1, -1):
            if up[k][u] != up[k][v]:
                u = up[k][u]
                v = up[k][v]
        return up[0][u]

    out = []
    append = out.append
    for i in range(Q):
        u = S[i]
        v = T[i]
        w = lca(u, v)
        m = node_value[w]
        ans = Ys[i] - Zs[i]
        if ans < 0:
            ans = -ans
        val = Ys[i] + Zs[i] - 2 * m
        if val > ans:
            ans = val
        append(str(ans))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Need check `del cells, dsu, sz, comp, find`: If we delete `dsu` while `find` closure exists, then `del find` in same statement. Is it okay to delete `find` after `dsu`? Yes. But if Python evaluates `del cells, dsu, sz, comp, find` left to right, it deletes `cells`, then `dsu`, then `sz`, then `comp`, then `find`. Deleting `dsu` clears the closure cell for `find`; then deleting `find` removes function. No error. If it deleted `find` first, no error. Good.

Potential issue: `del dsu` while `find` exists and `find` is a local variable; the closure cell for `dsu` is cleared. But if `find` is deleted after, no issue. However, if `del dsu` is executed and then `del find` but `find`'s closure cell is empty, deleting function doesn't access cell. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code if `depth[p]` where p is an internal node id and depth[p] already computed because p > i. We rely on parent id > child id. Is this always true? Yes. Let's prove once more: Leaves ids 0..N-1. Internal nodes created with ids N, N+1,... in increasing order. When an internal node is created, it becomes the parent of two component roots. Those component roots have ids < new (because they were created earlier or are leaves <N). Later, when this component merges into a larger component, its root (which may be this internal node or a later internal node if multiple merges) will have parent set to an even later internal node with larger id. Thus every parent id > child id. Therefore root is last created node id total_nodes-1. Good.

Final code with lists:

```
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    H, W = map(int, input().split())
    N = H * W
    cells = []
    for i in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        base = i * W
        for j, val in enumerate(row):
            cells.append((val, base + j))
    cells.sort(reverse=True)

    dsu = [-1] * N
    sz = [0] * N
    comp = [-1] * N
    max_nodes = 2 * N + 5
    node_value = [0] * max_nodes
    krt_parent = [-1] * max_nodes
    total_nodes = N

    def find(x):
        while dsu[x] != x:
            dsu[x] = dsu[dsu[x]]
            x = dsu[x]
        return x

    dirs = ((1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1))
    for h, v in cells:
        dsu[v] = v
        sz[v] = 1
        comp[v] = v
        node_value[v] = h
        r = v // W
        c = v - r * W
        for dr, dc in dirs:
            nr = r + dr
            nc = c + dc
            if 0 <= nr < H and 0 <= nc < W:
                nb = nr * W + nc
                if dsu[nb] != -1:
                    ru = find(nb)
                    rv = find(v)
                    if ru != rv:
                        new = total_nodes
                        total_nodes += 1
                        node_value[new] = h
                        child1 = comp[ru]
                        child2 = comp[rv]
                        krt_parent[child1] = new
                        krt_parent[child2] = new
                        if sz[ru] < sz[rv]:
                            dsu[ru] = rv
                            sz[rv] += sz[ru]
                            comp[rv] = new
                        else:
                            dsu[rv] = ru
                            sz[ru] += sz[rv]
                            comp[ru] = new

    del cells, dsu, sz, comp, find

    depth = [0] * total_nodes
    for i in range(total_nodes - 1, -1, -1):
        p = krt_parent[i]
        if p != -1:
            depth[i] = depth[p] + 1

    LOG = total_nodes.bit_length()
    up = [[0] * total_nodes for _ in range(LOG)]
    up0 = up[0]
    for i in range(total_nodes):
        p = krt_parent[i]
        up0[i] = p if p != -1 else i
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = up[k - 1]
        curr = up[k]
        for i in range(total_nodes):
            curr[i] = prev[prev[i]]

    del krt_parent

    Q = int(input())
    S = [0] * Q
    T = [0] * Q
    Ys = [0] * Q
    Zs = [0] * Q
    for qi in range(Q):
        A, B, Y, C, D, Z = map(int, input().split())
        S[qi] = (A - 1) * W + (B - 1)
        T[qi] = (C - 1) * W + (D - 1)
        Ys[qi] = Y
        Zs[qi] = Z

    def lca(u, v, depth=depth, up=up, LOG=LOG):
        if depth[u] < depth[v]:
            u, v = v, u
        diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
        bit = 0
        while diff:
            if diff & 1:
                u = up[bit][u]
            diff >>= 1
            bit += 1
        if u == v:
            return u
        for k in range(LOG - 1, -1, -1):
            if up[k][u] != up[k][v]:
                u = up[k][u]
                v = up[k][v]
        return up[0][u]

    out = []
    append = out.append
    for i in range(Q):
        u = S[i]
        v = T[i]
        w = lca(u, v)
        m = node_value[w]
        ans = Ys[i] - Zs[i]
        if ans < 0:
            ans = -ans
        val = Ys[i] + Zs[i] - 2 * m
        if val > ans:
            ans = val
        append(str(ans))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is final. Let's double-check `del cells, dsu, sz, comp, find` with `find` closure. If `del dsu` while `find` exists, the closure cell for dsu is cleared. Then `del find` in same statement. Is it valid to delete `dsu` when it's a cell variable? Yes. But let's be absolutely sure: In Python, if a nested function references a variable, that variable is stored in a cell. The `del` statement on a cell variable clears the cell. It is allowed. Example:
```
def outer():
    x=1
    def inner(): return x
    del x
```
This works; calling inner later raises NameError. So `del dsu` works. Then `del find` removes function. Good.

If we want to avoid any risk, we can not delete dsu before find? We can `del find` first, then `del dsu`. But in a single `del` statement, order is left to right? To be safe, do:
```
    del find
    del cells, dsu, sz, comp
```
But if we delete find first, its closure releases dsu? The function object is deleted, closure cell decref, but dsu local variable still references list. Then del dsu frees. This is safe. Let's do that to avoid any weirdness:
```
    del find
    del cells, dsu, sz, comp
```
This is clearer. But `find` is a local function; `del find` removes it. Then `del dsu` frees list. Good. Let's use this.

Similarly, after up build, `del krt_parent`.

Now, if we delete `find` before `dsu`, the closure cell for dsu is released, but dsu local still references list. Then del dsu. Good.

Need ensure when union by size attaches rv to ru, new internal node id `new` is > both child ids. child1=comp[ru], child2=comp[rv]. Are both child ids < new? comp[ru] and comp[rv] are either leaves (<N) or internal nodes created earlier (<new). Yes. Good.

Let's produce final code block.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline

    H, W = map(int, input().split())
    N = H * W

    cells = []
    for i in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        base = i * W
        for j, val in enumerate(row):
            cells.append((val, base + j))

    cells.sort(reverse=True)

    dsu = [-1] * N
    sz = [0] * N
    comp = [-1] * N

    max_nodes = 2 * N + 5
    node_value = [0] * max_nodes
    krt_parent = [-1] * max_nodes
    total_nodes = N

    def find(x):
        while dsu[x] != x:
            dsu[x] = dsu[dsu[x]]
            x = dsu[x]
        return x

    dirs = ((1, 0), (-1, 0), (0, 1), (0, -1))

    for h, v in cells:
        dsu[v] = v
        sz[v] = 1
        comp[v] = v
        node_value[v] = h

        r = v // W
        c = v - r * W

        for dr, dc in dirs:
            nr = r + dr
            nc = c + dc
            if 0 <= nr < H and 0 <= nc < W:
                nb = nr * W + nc
                if dsu[nb] != -1:
                    ru = find(nb)
                    rv = find(v)
                    if ru != rv:
                        new = total_nodes
                        total_nodes += 1

                        node_value[new] = h

                        child1 = comp[ru]
                        child2 = comp[rv]
                        krt_parent[child1] = new
                        krt_parent[child2] = new

                        if sz[ru] < sz[rv]:
                            dsu[ru] = rv
                            sz[rv] += sz[ru]
                            comp[rv] = new
                        else:
                            dsu[rv] = ru
                            sz[ru] += sz[rv]
                            comp[ru] = new

    del find
    del cells, dsu, sz, comp

    depth = [0] * total_nodes
    for i in range(total_nodes - 1, -1, -1):
        p = krt_parent[i]
        if p != -1:
            depth[i] = depth[p] + 1

    LOG = total_nodes.bit_length()
    up = [[0] * total_nodes for _ in range(LOG)]

    up0 = up[0]
    for i in range(total_nodes):
        p = krt_parent[i]
        up0[i] = p if p != -1 else i

    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = up[k - 1]
        curr = up[k]
        for i in range(total_nodes):
            curr[i] = prev[prev[i]]

    del krt_parent

    Q = int(input())
    S = [0] * Q
    T = [0] * Q
    Ys = [0] * Q
    Zs = [0] * Q

    for qi in range(Q):
        A, B, Y, C, D, Z = map(int, input().split())
        S[qi] = (A - 1) * W + (B - 1)
        T[qi] = (C - 1) * W + (D - 1)
        Ys[qi] = Y
        Zs[qi] = Z

    def lca(u, v, depth=depth, up=up, LOG=LOG):
        if depth[u] < depth[v]:
            u, v = v, u

        diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
        bit = 0
        while diff:
            if diff & 1:
                u = up[bit][u]
            diff >>= 1
            bit += 1

        if u == v:
            return u

        for k in range(LOG - 1, -1, -1):
            if up[k][u] != up[k][v]:
                u = up[k][u]
                v = up[k][v]

        return up[0][u]

    out = []
    append = out.append

    for i in range(Q):
        w = lca(S[i], T[i])
        m = node_value[w]

        ans = abs(Ys[i] - Zs[i])
        val = Ys[i] + Zs[i] - 2 * m
        if val > ans:
            ans = val

        append(str(ans))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
